# Verification log

This log is the record of every game rule, constant, API signature and tool behavior the bot depends on (SPEC §0).
Rules: append or supersede rows and never delete them. Use the API constant where one exists. Everything else goes in
`crates/rustedscreep-consts/src/extra.rs` with its source and a "last verified" date.

**Confidence levels**
- `verified`: read directly in a priority 1–3 source (crate docs or source for the pinned version, docs.screeps.com, or engine source) that applies to the version or server in use.
- `inferred`: derived from verified facts, read in a priority 4–5 source, read through a summarizing tool rather than verbatim, or read for a different version.
- `unverified`: not found yet. The row states the assumption and the safe default.

Columns: # | Group | Parameter | Finding | Applies to | Source | Date | Confidence | Constant / code location

---

The loader then runs `require(<module>)` to get the bytes, `new WebAssembly.Module(bytes)`, then `initSync({ module })` across ticks, frees the bytes and deletes the require cache, and swaps in the loop. A `running` flag left set means the previous tick aborted, so the loader calls `Game.cpu.halt()` (wasm-bindgen issue #3130). try/catch logs only the message, since the panic hook prints the stack. `console.error` is routed to `Game.notify`. | starter main `js_src/main.js` | https://raw.githubusercontent.com/rustyscreeps/screeps-starter-rust/main/js_src/main.js | 2026-11-13 | inferred (read through a summarizing fetch; re-read raw); superseded 2026-11-13 → V17 | js/src/main.ts, ADR-003 |

## C. Discrepancies with SPEC

| # | SPEC ref | SPEC says | Finding | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | §1 tech stack | "`wasm-pack build --target web` (or `cargo-screeps`)" | cargo-screeps doesn't work with screeps-game-api ≥0.22 (S3) | Drop cargo-screeps (ADR-001). Confirmed 2026-11-13 (V12). |
| D2 | §1 TS glue 3 | "re-instantiate on the next tick" | The starter halts the VM with `Game.cpu.halt()` after an aborted tick because of wasm-bindgen #3130 (S5) | ADR-003 (c), the hybrid. P1 ships halt mode (a): the loop runs again 3 ticks after a trap, the recovery bound the user set on 2026-11-14 (ADR-003), met by 10 traps in a row on the MMO (V148). Next-tick re-instantiation is mode (b), which waits for its own 10-trap MMO run. |
| D3 | §1 release profile | `panic="abort"` (confirm compatibility) | Compatible only because recovery lives in JS. Unwinding needs nightly and engine support (S7) | Keep abort. Confirmed 2026-11-13: unwinding needs nightly and build-std (V26), which the stable-only policy (PROJECT_CONTEXT) rules out. |
| D4 | §5 code quality | "Per-room and per-creep error isolation" | Under abort, `catch_unwind` can't isolate. Isolation has to come from `Result` boundaries | Recorded as an architecture invariant |
| D5 | §4b VERIFY | Can dismantle combine with other actions? | Dismantle is blocked by heal, rangedHeal and attackController. It can run alongside move and rangedAttack (V44; derived in V56) | Breaker never heals while dismantling. Confirmed 2026-11-13 at engine a90d26f (V44, V56); a partner healing the breaker does not block its dismantle. |
| D6 | §0 tooling | wasm-pack as the build tool | The rustwasm org is archived and wasm-pack has a new maintainer (S8) | ADR-001 compares wasm-pack with the wasm-bindgen CLI plus wasm-opt. Confirmed 2026-11-13 (V6): wasm-pack is maintained at wasm-bindgen/wasm-pack (v0.15.1). ADR-001 accepted 2026-11-13: the wasm-bindgen CLI plus a pinned wasm-opt, not wasm-pack. |
| D7 | §1 release profile | "strip" | Cargo's `strip = true` means "symbols", which drops the `target_features` section, and wasm-bindgen 0.2.130 then cannot detect the module's wasm features (V81) | `strip = "debuginfo"` in the root release profile (P1 module 1, 2026-11-13): drops only `.debug_*` sections. Module 3 (2026-11-14): `wasm-opt -O4` drops the name section anyway unless given `-g` (V91), so the shipped .wasm has no function names either way and `strip` shapes only the intermediate file. |
| D8 | §1 tech stack | "`log`/`fern`" | The bot uses one fern output (`console.log`) and none of its features, and fern costs 47140 bytes of release wasm, 28% of the module (V126) | ADR-014 (accepted 2026-11-14, "Yes, drop fern"): rustedscreep-wasm has its own minimal `log::Log` in `logging.rs`; `log` stays (V128). |

## E. Remaining unverified items (closing list; maintained from P12)

Open from P2.4, the RCL1–3 opening (2026-11-15). Each item gives the safe assumption to work on until it is read, and how to settle it.

- `createConstructionSite` billing: settled 2026-11-15 → V203 (0.2 CPU per accepted call; the code text verbatim, the line numbers inferred).
- Same-tick RCL counting of construction sites: filled 2026-11-15 → V204 (as assumed: the game side does not count the tick's earlier sites, the processor drops the excess silently; inferred from summaries except the processor's quoted L19-33). The interim placement calls once per room per tick (ADR-020). Check: rooms.js byte-exact.
- The exit-adjacent rule of `checkConstructionSite`: filled 2026-11-15 → V205 (inferred: x or y of 1 or 48 beside a non-wall edge tile, every type but road and container); still open: the corner tiles such as (1, 1), the `CONSTRUCTION_COST` key list for sources and minerals, and whether ERR_FULL counts the sites of all rooms. The interim placement keeps x and y in 3..47, clear of all of it. Check: utils.js L128-189 byte-exact.
- `calcBodyEffectiveness` and boosts (V183, V197): its skip rule is read, its boost arithmetic is not, so boosted harvest, build, upgrade and carry capacity stay open (R21, P3). Check: utils.js L623-636 byte-exact.
- The first visible `ticksToLive` (V198): assume undefined while spawning and on the first tick outside the spawn, read as a full life. Check: log a new creep's `ticksToLive` over its first ticks on the MMO. Since P5.2 the snapshot reads it (V261): as an `Option`, again on each tick while it is undefined, and keeps `Game.time` plus the first number, which is `ageTime` (game/creeps.js L77) whichever tick that number first shows, so the bot's death tick is exact either way. What stays open is only whether a creep lives `CREEP_LIFE_TIME` or one tick less once out of the spawn: the world model in economy/tests.rs, opening-sim's `ageTime` and the chain model in `spawning::replace` assume the longer one, so they would run one tick long. The live check is in TEST_PLAN §8 (pre-spawn replacement).
- The order of processing within a tick (V202): one summarized read. Assume all intents, then the per-object tick pass. Check: processor.js byte-exact. Sources and containers settled 2026-11-16 → V215 (the intents at L229-323, sources/tick.js at L353, containers/tick.js at L389); the rest stays open.
- Container decay in an owned room (V211, R15; P3.1, ADR-023): assume `CONTAINER_DECAY` per `CONTAINER_DECAY_TIME_OWNED`, so a new container lasts 25000 ticks unrepaired, longer than P3.5 needs to arrive. Check: the processor's container tick before P3.5 repairs anything. Settled 2026-11-16 → V214 (engine a90d26f, live reads agree): the first step `CONTAINER_DECAY_TIME` − 1 ticks after the build in any room, then every `CONTAINER_DECAY_TIME_OWNED` − 1 while the room's controller has a level, so about 24550 ticks unrepaired in an owned room and 4950 elsewhere.
- Drop mining through drop.js (V212): assume the harvest overflow of a creep with no CARRY reaches the container or pile on its tile in full. Check: live, a miner's container fills by about 10 energy a tick (TEST_PLAN §8), and creeps/drop.js byte-exact. The live check passed 2026-11-16 → V213 (10 a tick on four consecutive ticks); drop.js stays open.
- The link network (P3.2, V216 to V220): creeps/withdraw.js settled 2026-11-16 → V220 (byte-exact: it clamps to the creep's room and the link's energy). Live (TEST_PLAN §8, from RCL5): a send's receiver gains the amount less `Math.ceil(amount * 0.03)` and the sender shows a cooldown of its range less 1 on the next tick (V217); no send is refused.
- Smaller points from the same pass: when a new extension gets its energy capacity (V186); that a completed structure is visible on the next tick (V186); creeps/drop.js's checks (V193); _die.js past L80 (V194); the game-side ruin data and the store layout per object (V192, V195, V196); the order of RESOURCES_ALL in the damage drop (V197); the game side's exact controller test in `harvest` (V182); the gating of `Game.constructionSites` (V199); the crate getters read by one reader (V201); and the engine rows of V182-V202 as a whole, which no one read byte-exact (a local clone of a90d26f with line numbers would settle them).
- The terrain corpus on the MMO (P4.1, V224 to V229, V231): the generator and backend rows are open-source reads (summarized), and the official server's code is closed. The corpus data already settles the digit order and the objects' placement (V233). Still open: that digit i of the encoded string is byte i of `getRawBuffer` on the MMO (V226). Assume it (the border test's row-major match makes a different layout unlikely). Check: the console hash in TEST_PLAN §8 for E7N57 and E3N53.

## F. P0 kickoff verification (2026-11-13; supersedes section A and fills section B rows as marked)

Method: six screeps-verifier researchers (tooling versions, starter, Rust/wasm, cpu-memory, pipeline, auxiliary tooling), then independent skeptics who re-read the load-bearing sources verbatim (curl, pinned to commit SHAs or tags). A row is `verified` only where a skeptic confirmed it from a verbatim read, or where it was read directly from a structured API. Pins used: screeps-game-api 0.23.5 (a1acd82), screeps/engine a90d26f, screeps/driver cf63d8a (package.json 5.3.0; no git tag), screeps/common 2fb779b, screeps/docs 142f15e, screeps-starter-rust 0aab129, binaryen version_133, Rust 1.100.0.

| V17 | tooling | (supersedes S5) Starter loader sequence | `MODULE_NAME = "screeps_starter_rust"`, `BUCKET_BOOT_THRESHOLD = 1500`. Boot loop: below the threshold, log "startup deferred" and return. Otherwise run `wasm_bytes = require(MODULE_NAME)`, `wasm_module = new WebAssembly.Module(wasm_bytes)`, `bot.initSync({ module: wasm_module })`. Each stage is guarded by an `if`, with no early return, so normally all three run in ONE tick; the guards only let a later tick resume after a throw or kill. Then the loader frees the bytes, deletes `require.cache[MODULE_NAME]` and swaps in `loaded_loop`. `loaded_loop`: `console.error` is overridden to log and `Game.notify`; `if (running) Game.cpu.halt(); else try { running = true; bot.loop(); running = false } catch { console.log(...) }`. The catch does not reset `running`, so a thrown trap also halts on the next tick (wasm-bindgen #3130). The bundle imports a TextEncoder/TextDecoder polyfill (`fastestsmallesttextencoderdecoder-encodeinto`). | starter @0aab129 | https://raw.githubusercontent.com/rustyscreeps/screeps-starter-rust/0aab1296a8f0c146af6b0ba4f2e350d418112abd/js_src/main.js | 2026-11-13 | verified (skeptic read raw) | js/src/main.ts, ADR-003 |
| V18 | tooling | (fills R3) `--target` and instantiate pattern | `--target web` emits `initSync` and an async default init in `pkg/<name>.js`. | V19 | tooling | (fills R5 in part) Upload format and module naming | `api.userCodeSet({ branch, modules })`. A `.wasm` file is sent as `{ binary: <base64> }` and JS as a UTF-8 string. It was raised from 2 MB in 2019 (commit f15806bf). No module-count limit. The MMO backend is closed source, so its limit is unverified. Assumption: same 5 MiB. Driver runtime.js L60-61: `intentCpu = 0.2`, `freeMethods = {say, pull}`. `set` charges only the first write per object+method (last write wins); `push`/`pushByName` charge each and return false without charge at maxLen; `remove` (cancelOrder) refunds 0.2. make.js L193-194 bills `ceil(usedCleanTime + 0.2 * count(final intent list))`, so canceled intents are not billed and intents the processor later rejects are. The crate exports `INTENT_CPU_COST = 0.2`. | docs @142f15e; driver cf63d8a; crate 0.23.5 | https://github.com/screeps/docs/blob/142f15e85edeafcf0b7311311038a9e8ef66712e/api/scripts/tag-api_method.js#L16 ; https://github.com/screeps/driver/blob/cf63d8adf902663e2ebddd7f8c5b7baa425dc928/lib/runtime/runtime.js#L60-L99 ; .../lib/runtime/make.js#L193-L194 | 2026-11-13 | verified (skeptic read raw) | `screeps::constants::INTENT_CPU_COST`; act layer skips no-op intents |
| V35 | cpu-memory | (fills R6) CPU limit | Docs: "fixed at 20" without CPU Unlock; with it, 20 plus 10 per GCL up to 300 (control.md L8; its image shows GCL 10 = 120 CPU). The engine reads `Game.cpu.limit = runtimeData.user.cpu`, set by the backend. Dev update 2020-03-08: recurring subscriptions ended, active ones stay, 24-hour tokens replaced 60-day ones. | V74 | tooling | Steam client local-folder sync | Read from the installed client (package.nw, packageVersion 248, built 2026-10-30; module app.nw-local-file-sync). The folder is fixed, not chosen: `<app data>/scripts/<world>/<branch>/` (this machine: `~/.config/Screeps/scripts/screeps.com/`, one subfolder per code branch, shared by all MMO shards). There is no folder picker; "Open local folder" only opens it. Only top-level `*.js` and `*.wasm` files sync (non-recursive); module name = file name minus the extension; `.wasm` is uploaded as `{binary: base64}`. Sync is two-way: local add/change/unlink uploads the whole branch at once (no debounce), so deleting a file deletes the module and an empty folder clears the branch; a code change from elsewhere (for example an API deploy) prompts to replace the local files, or writes them silently if "Always replace" was chosen; in-game editor commits are written locally at once; a server-to-local write deletes local files the server lacks. Creating or deleting a branch folder creates or deletes the server branch, and a startup reconcile can delete remote branches that have no local folder. The client writes a mirrored `.wasm` back as UTF-8 text, which corrupts every byte >= 0x80 (reproduced with Node), and a later local change re-uploads the damaged file. | Steam client packageVersion 248 | installed client package (build.min.js: app.nw-local-file-sync, app.code-branches) ; https://screeps.com/forum/post/9025 (staff, 2017: Steam client can upload .wasm) | 2026-11-13 | verified (client code read); inferred (MMO server side, from backend-local) | never write, copy or link anything into the mirror (ADR-012) |

It calls `POST user/github-repo {repo, folder}`, and its error text expects a `default` branch (client code). Unknown: whether pushes to `main` trigger it, whether `.wasm` is copied, and whether missing files are deleted. Assumptions and safe defaults until the P1 test: the sync replaces the `default` branch with the folder's top-level files, so the folder holds only a staged build that passed `/check`; modules not in the folder are deleted; every push reloads the code (global reset plus wasm compile, costing bucket); a `.wasm` is suspect until its hash matches. P1 test, using the first checked build staged by `/deploy mmo` (the user names mmo and pushes): (1) before the push, the user records the `default` module list and adds a sentinel module in the in-game editor that is not in `deploy/`; (2) after the push, the loader logs its build ID, `Game.time`, the type of the value `require()` returned and a hash over all its bytes, which must equal the hash `npm run stage` reported (a real wasm build contains bytes >= 0x80; a length or validate check alone can miss corruption); (3) whether the sentinel survived shows the deletion behavior; (4) the first logged `Game.time` against the push time gives the delay; (5) a docs-only push shows whether an identical re-sync logs a new boot. | client GitHub integration; repo jabbott-iii/rustedscreep, branch main | the user's observation (2026-11-13); V75 (client code and community reports) | 2026-11-13 | verified (trigger on `main`, user-observed); unverified (`.wasm`, deletion, delay, re-sync); `.wasm` part superseded 2026-11-14 → V136; deletion, delay and re-sync superseded 2026-11-14 → V145 | P1 gate; ADR-012 |

## G. P1 verification (2026-11-13 onward)

Method: per module, screeps-verifier researchers, then a skeptic who re-reads each cited source and tries to refute it. Local probes run on the pinned toolchain in a scratch copy of the workspace, never in the repo. A row is `verified` where a skeptic confirmed it from a read source, or where a local probe showed it.

| # | Group | Parameter | Finding | Applies to | Source | Date | Confidence | Constant / code location |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V78 | tooling | Cargo virtual workspace: resolver and default-members | A virtual manifest has no package edition, so `[workspace] resolver` must be set. Without it a virtual workspace defaults to resolver "1". Resolver "3" (needs Rust 1.84+; the edition 2024 default) sets `incompatible-rust-versions = "fallback"`, which prefers dependency versions whose `rust-version` is at most the workspace's. `default-members` applies when no `-p` or `--workspace` flag is given. Local probe: a bare `cargo build` in the root compiled only rustedscreep-consts and rustedscreep-core, and `cargo clippy -p rustedscreep-wasm` still reached the cdylib. | cargo 1.100.0 | https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/workspaces.html ; https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/resolver.html ; local probe | 2026-11-13 | verified (skeptic re-read; probe) | root `Cargo.toml` `[workspace]` |
| V79 | tooling | Profiles and inherited package keys | `[profile.*]`, `[patch]` and `[replace]` are read only from the workspace root and ignored in members. Whether cargo warns about a member profile is unverified. `[workspace.package]` keys (among them version, edition, rust-version, license and publish) are inherited per key with `key.workspace = true` (Rust 1.64+). | cargo 1.100.0 | https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/workspaces.html ; https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/profiles.html | 2026-11-13 | verified (skeptic re-read) | root `Cargo.toml`; `crates/*/Cargo.toml` |
| V80 | tooling | `panic` setting and profile inheritance | Tests, benchmarks, build scripts and proc macros ignore `panic`, so they unwind although release sets "abort". The `bench` profile inherits `release` (fat LTO and one codegen unit, so bench builds are slow), and `test` inherits `dev`. | cargo 1.100.0 | https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/profiles.html | 2026-11-13 | verified (skeptic re-read) | root `[profile.release]` |
| V81 | tooling | `strip` on wasm32 with wasm-bindgen | Cargo `strip` takes "none", "debuginfo" or "symbols", and `true` means "symbols". rustc's WasmLd linker passes `--strip-debug` for debuginfo and `--strip-all` for symbols. Without the `target_features` section, wasm-bindgen 0.2.130 cannot detect the module's wasm features (multivalue is read only from that section). If `__externref_table_alloc` is exported, it then assumes reference types and prints a warning ("likely due to strip = true") that advises against `strip = true`. Otherwise it leaves reference types off without a warning. Local probe (release cdylib with one export, Rust 1.100.0): "symbols" left no custom sections (66 bytes); "debuginfo" kept `name`, `producers` and `target_features` and dropped only `.debug_*` (339 bytes); "none" kept `.debug_*` too (82175 bytes). Not checked yet: whether `__wasm_bindgen_unstable` survives "symbols", and whether wasm-opt `-O4` drops the name section (P1 module 3; the name-section question was answered 2026-11-14 by V91: `-O4` drops it unless `-g`). | Rust 1.100.0; wasm-bindgen 0.2.130 | https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/profiles.html ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/rust-lang/rust/1.100.0/compiler/rustc_codegen_ssa/src/back/linker.rs ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/wasm-bindgen/wasm-bindgen/0.2.130/crates/cli-support/src/lib.rs ; local probe | 2026-11-13 | verified (skeptic re-read; probe) | root `[profile.release] strip = "debuginfo"` (D7) |
| V82 | tooling | `rust-toolchain.toml` and rustup auto-install | `[toolchain]` takes channel, profile, components and targets (`path` excludes `channel`). A full version such as "1.100.0" is a valid channel, and components add to the profile. `RUSTUP_AUTO_INSTALL` defaults to 1, so a missing active toolchain is installed on first use: 1.28.0 removed auto-install, 1.28.1 restored it, and 1.28.2 added `rustup set auto-install disable`. `RUSTUP_AUTO_INSTALL=0` turns it off. Whether auto-install also adds listed components and targets to an installed toolchain is unverified. The 1.100.0 channel manifest marks clippy, rustfmt and rust-std for wasm32-unknown-unknown available. Local: `rustup toolchain install 1.100.0 --profile minimal --component rustfmt,clippy --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` installed rustc 1.100.0 (0ef1bc386 2026-11-09), clippy 0.1.100, rustfmt 1.11.0 and the wasm32 std. rustup updated itself from 1.29.2 to 1.29.3 during that command. Local probe (review skeptic, offline dist server): with a toolchain file naming an uninstalled channel, `cargo --version` started an install without any prompt, and with `RUSTUP_AUTO_INSTALL=0` it stopped with "toolchain ... is not installed". So a plain cargo command installs a missing pinned toolchain without asking. Since 2026-11-13 `.claude/settings.json` sets `RUSTUP_AUTO_INSTALL=0` for Claude Code sessions in this repo; a local check with it set gave exit 1 and "toolchain '1.999.0-x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu' is not installed" for an uninstalled channel, and cargo 1.100.0 as usual in the repo. | rustup 1.29.3; Rust 1.100.0 | https://rust-lang.github.io/rustup/overrides.html ; https://rust-lang.github.io/rustup/environment-variables.html ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/rust-lang/rustup/master/CHANGELOG.md ; https://static.rust-lang.org/dist/channel-rust-1.100.0.toml ; local install output | 2026-11-13 | verified (docs, manifest, install); unverified (component auto-add) | `rust-toolchain.toml`; `.claude/settings.json` `env` |
| V83 | tooling | No-panic clippy lints | clippy 0.1.100 knows unwrap_used, expect_used, panic, indexing_slicing, todo, unimplemented and unreachable: the crate roots pass `-D warnings`, and a planted unknown `clippy::` name fails with "unknown lint". panic, todo, unimplemented, unreachable and indexing_slicing are restriction lints (read at master; the group of unwrap_used and expect_used was not re-read). `panic` flags `panic!` and `panic_any`, not `assert!`, and todo, unimplemented and unreachable are separate lints. `indexing_slicing` flags `x[i]` on slices and Vecs, but not a constant in-bounds index into a fixed-size array or `[..]`, and it skips proc-macro code. None of the seven covers the `assert!` family, division by zero or overflow. clippy.toml has `allow-{unwrap,expect,panic,indexing-slicing}-in-tests` (default false). Local probe: one planted violation per lint gave exactly 7 errors in rustedscreep-core (native) and in rustedscreep-wasm (wasm32), and a constant index into `[u8; 3]` gave none. | clippy 0.1.100 (Rust 1.100.0); lint details read at rust-clippy master | https://raw.githubusercontent.com/rust-lang/rust-clippy/master/clippy_lints/src/panic_unimplemented.rs ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/rust-lang/rust-clippy/master/clippy_lints/src/indexing_slicing.rs ; https://doc.rust-lang.org/clippy/lint_configuration.html ; local probe | 2026-11-13 | verified (names and firing, by probe); inferred (other lint details, read at master, not the 1.100 tag) | crate roots of all three crates |
| V84 | tooling | `cfg_attr(not(test), deny(...))` under `--all-targets` | Local probe: the same planted violations inside a `#[cfg(test)]` module pass `cargo clippy -p rustedscreep-core --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and outside it they fail. The lib build gets the denies and the unit-test build does not. Integration tests, benches and examples are separate crates without the attribute (inferred). Not checked yet: whether `#[wasm_bindgen]` expansions trip these lints (P1 module 4; for a plain function export, closed 2026-11-14 by V94; extern blocks and exported types are still open). | clippy 0.1.100 | local probe | 2026-11-13 | verified (probe) | crate roots |
| V85 | cpu-memory | RawMemory segment ids (P1.2) | Valid ids are integers 0 to 99, so there are 100 segments. `setActiveSegments` rejects more than 10 ids, runs `parseInt` on each and rejects NaN, > 99 and < 0 (numeric strings pass). `setPublicSegments`, `setDefaultPublicSegment` (unless null) and `setActiveForeignSegment` (unless undefined) run the same `parseInt` and range check. The end-of-tick save throws, in this order, on more than 10 keys, a key outside 0 to 99, a value that is not a string ("Memory segment #N is not a string") and a value longer than 100*1024 characters, then stores `"" + value`. The driver uses only the literals 0, 99 and 10, with no named constant. screeps-game-api 0.23.5 exports `MEMORY_SEGMENT_SIZE_LIMIT` and `MEMORY_SEGMENT_ACTIVE_LIMIT` but nothing for the id range; its segment functions take `u8` ids, and the range appears only in a doc comment (raw_memory.rs:93). | driver cf63d8a; crate 0.23.5 | https://raw.githubusercontent.com/screeps/driver/cf63d8adf902663e2ebddd7f8c5b7baa425dc928/lib/runtime/runtime.js (setters L129-L188, end-of-tick save L250-L269; re-read with curl) ; ~/.cargo/registry/src/.../screeps-game-api-0.23.5/src/constants/extra.rs:153,161 and src/raw_memory.rs:54,93,96 | 2026-11-13 | verified (researcher, skeptic, and a curl read of the pinned file) | `rustedscreep_consts::extra::MEMORY_SEGMENT_COUNT` |
| V86 | tooling | Pure, JS-backed and panicking items in `screeps::local` (P1.2) | Pure: `RoomName` (parse, Display, `checked_add`, packed form), `RoomCoordinate`, `RoomOffset`, `RoomXY` and `Position` (constructors, packed forms and their checked arithmetic), `LocalCostMatrix` (get, set, Index, into `Vec<u8>`), `LocalRoomTerrain` (`new_from_bits`, `get_xy`, `get_bits`), `XMajor`, `YMajor`, `xy_to_linear_index` and `xy_to_terrain_index`. JS-backed: `Position`'s 9 game methods (create_construction_site, create_flag, find_closest_by_path, find_closest_by_range, find_in_range, find_path_to, find_path_to_xy, look, look_for), `From<RoomPosition>`, the `RoomName` to and from `JsValue`/`JsString` conversions, `LocalRoomTerrain` from `RoomTerrain`, `LocalCostMatrix` from `&CostMatrix`, `ObjectId`'s resolve methods and JS conversions, `RawObjectId`'s `TryFrom<JsString>` (the type itself is a pure u128), and `LodashFilter`. On a native target a wasm-bindgen import compiles to a stub that panics ("cannot call wasm-bindgen imported functions on non-wasm targets"). Panics or overflows on bad input: `Position::from_packed` and `Position`'s binary (non-human-readable) serde deserialization assert x and y < `ROOM_SIZE`; `linear_index_to_xy` and `terrain_index_to_xy` assert idx < `ROOM_AREA`; `RoomXY`'s and `Position`'s `+`/`-` with a tuple or `Direction`, their `offset`, `Position::from_world_coords` and `RoomName`'s `+`/`-` panic out of bounds (checked forms: `checked_add`, `checked_add_direction`, `checked_from_world_coords`, `RoomXY`'s `saturating_*`); `RoomXY::towards`, `between` and `midpoint_between` multiply `i8` offsets, which overflows for inputs such as (0,0) towards (40,0) by 10, and divide by zero for equal points; `Position::towards` and `between` divide by zero when the points are equal and the distance is 0 or less. The `unwrap_throw` calls in `RoomCoordinate`/`RoomOffset` arithmetic act only on values already clamped into range. Layouts: `LocalCostMatrix` and `xy_to_linear_index` are column-major (`x * 50 + y`); `LocalRoomTerrain` and `xy_to_terrain_index` are row-major (`y * 50 + x`). `get_xy` reads 0b01 and 0b11 as Wall, 0b10 as Swamp and 0b00 as Plain. | V96 | tooling | Screeps runtime behavior the loader relies on (P1.4) | Open-source driver and engine (the MMO backend is closed): `Game.cpu.halt()` disposes the isolate and throws, so the halting tick's intents and Memory writes are discarded; the next tick builds a new isolate and evaluates main again with fresh globals. `halt` exists only when the runtime supplies it. Hitting the CPU limit throws `Script execution timed out: CPU time limit reached` from that evalCode call, but the isolate and globals are kept and that tick's Memory and intents are still saved, so a module-level `running` flag survives; a separate wall-clock guard (max(5000 ms, mainLoopResetInterval)) resets the VM. `console` is rebuilt before every tick with `log`, `logUnsafe`, `commandResult`, `addVisual`, `getVisualSize`, `clearVisual` and `getVisual`: no `error` or `warn`, and only `log` and `logUnsafe` are writable. `Game.notify`: at most 20 per tick; the driver keeps the first 500 characters (the docs say 1000) and clamps `groupInterval` to 0 to 1440 minutes. `global` is the context's global. Console commands run after `main.loop` and are skipped when loop throws. `Memory` is a getter that parses on first access each tick and is then redefined read-only; it is saved at end of tick only if accessed and not null (mutate fields, never reassign). When the raw string is not valid JSON the getter's catch sets it to `null` (engine game.js L482-499), so code must not assume an object. `require` of a missing module throws `Unknown module '<name>'`. `Game.cpu.getUsed()` counts from just before `game.run`, so it includes module evaluation, the compile inside `require('main')`, and intent CPU. js/tools/smoke.mjs follows these rules. | driver cf63d8a; engine a90d26f; MMO inferred | https://raw.githubusercontent.com/screeps/driver/cf63d8adf902663e2ebddd7f8c5b7baa425dc928/lib/runtime/runtime.js ; .../lib/runtime/user-vm.js ; .../lib/runtime/make.js ; .../lib/runtime/runtime-driver.js ; .../lib/index.js ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/screeps/engine/a90d26fc9eb943147799e65d0bf9d4d0d79a2722/src/game/game.js ; .../src/game/console.js ; https://docs.screeps.com/api/#Game.cpu | 2026-11-14 | verified (open-source source, researcher and skeptic; function-level anchors from summarized reads); inferred (MMO) | `js/src/main.ts`, `js/src/loader.ts`, `js/src/env.d.ts`, `js/tools/smoke.mjs` |

The trap-test export has a narrow `#[allow(clippy::panic, reason = ...)]`, exists in every build, and panics only with `--features trap-test`. Sandbox (node:vm with the V96 rules, Node 18.19.1; times are local milliseconds, not MMO CPU): the 18,474-byte wasm booted over 2 ticks, with load and CRC-32 about 2 ms, compile about 1 ms, instantiate about 0.2 ms and the first loop 0.03 ms. Ten forced traps (`rustedscreep.trapTest(10)` on a trap-test build): halt mode recovered each in 3 ticks (trap, halt, compile, then instantiate and run; 11 globals) and reinstantiate mode each in 2 ticks with no halt (1 global); each panic message reached the routed console.error, and one summary notification was sent. A flipped byte in the wasm still compiled and ran, and only the CRC-32 caught it; a truncated wasm gave compile incidents and backoff without a halt; a corrupt loader record (including a malformed trap-test run) was repaired and the bot booted; a `Memory` that is not a JSON object (null, or a number) gave a heap record and one notification; without `Game.cpu.halt`, halt mode fell back to re-instantiation and, when capped, retried after the window instead of stopping. Under a persistent trap in halt mode the gap between boots grew 3, 3, 101, 201, 401, 801, 1601 and then 3201 ticks (the cap). Sizes: `dist/main.js` 35,255 bytes, wasm 18,474 bytes, upload estimate 60,869 of 2,097,152 characters. | V100 | tooling | Boundary semantics the snapshot relies on (P1.5): wasm-bindgen 0.2.130, js-sys 0.3.107, screeps-game-api 0.23.5 | Numeric import returns: the release glue converts nothing (`assert_number` is debug-only) and Rust casts the raw wasm i32, so the host's ToInt32 decides: undefined, null, NaN and ±Infinity give 0 for u32/i32, fractions truncate, u8 truncates further; f64 passes through (undefined gives NaN); bool is `value != 0`; `Option<u32>` maps undefined and null to None via a sentinel. Consequence: an unlimited user's `Game.cpu.limit` and `bucket` (Infinity, V37) read as 0 through the crate's `limit() -> u32` and `bucket() -> i32`; the MMO reports finite values. Imported types and `JsString` returns have no runtime type check (instanceof only on `dyn_*`). `JsValue::as_string()` returns None for a non-string; `String::from(JsString)` throws through `unwrap_throw`. Reading an id or name as `JsString` and then `as_string()` costs two imports per value: the getter and `__wbindgen_string_get`, which is the type check. A getter typed `-> Option<String>` would be one import, but a review agent's Node probe against this build reports that a non-string then reaches `passStringToWasm0` with an undefined length and ends in `RuntimeError: memory access out of bounds` (reported, not re-run here), so the snapshot keeps the checked form. Passing an ASCII string JS to Rust is one `__wbindgen_malloc` call plus a JS loop into memory (no realloc, no encodeInto), giving an owned String. Dropping a JsValue runs `__externref_table_dealloc` inside wasm, with no JS call. `Object::values`, `Array::get` and `Array::length` are one JS call each, with no Rust-heap allocation. Getters are structural by default (plain property access), and a `static_method_of = Game, getter` binding reads the global `Game` on every call. Panicking conveniences in the crate: `HasId::raw_id` and `MaybeHasId::try_raw_id` (expect on parse), `Room::name` and RoomName's `JsCollectionFromValue` (expect), `JsHashMap::entries` (tuple expect), `Position::from_packed` and `From<RoomPosition>` (assert x, y < 50; room bits unchecked), the RoomPosition room-name getter (expect); `StructureObject::from` reads `structureType` for every object. `OwnedStructure::my()` maps an undefined or null `my` to false. | V103 | tooling | Packed positions and object ids (P1.5) | `RoomPosition.__packedPos` = `(roomX + 128) << 24 \| (roomY + 128) << 16 \| x << 8 \| y`, with W<n> as roomX = -n-1, E<n> as n, N<n> as roomY = -n-1 and S<n> as n; screeps-game-api's `Position` uses the same bits (its docs say it copies the engine). Worked examples: W1N1 (25, 10) = 0x7E7E190A, E0S0 (25, 10) = 0x8080190A. The engine computes it with JS `<<` and `\|`, so the value is a signed int32 and negative for E and S rooms (a review agent read this in an installed @screeps/engine 4.3.0, src/game/rooms.js:1288-1291); the wasm boundary keeps the same 32 bits (V100), so `packed() -> u32` and a local `Option<u32>` getter read the crate's layout either way. Every 16-bit room value decodes to a `RoomName`; only x, y < 50 needs checking. (a native test decodes all 65536 room values and re-parses each name). `RawObjectId::from_str` packs `(hex value << 32) \| string length` (value < 2^96, length ≤ 24), so ids that differ only by leading zeros stay distinct; it also accepts uppercase digits and a leading `+` (Rust's `from_str_radix`), which would alias real ids. Ids: MongoDB ObjectIds (24 lowercase hex) on the MMO (inferred; the backend is closed); a private server's storage `genId` builds a shorter random-plus-timestamp hex id (inferred, read through a fetch tool at storage master, not pinned). The first char must be `E`/`e` or `W`/`w`. The second char starts the x text unconditionally, and the first `N`/`n`/`S`/`s` from the third char ends it. The x text (`s[start..end]`) and the rest after that letter (`s[start..]`) are each parsed with `i32::from_str`, so one leading `+` or `-` and any leading zeros are accepted (`E-5N5` is W4N5). `W`n and `N`n map to -n-1 (L402-403), and both coordinates must lie in -128..128 (`VALID_ROOM_NAME_COORDINATES`, `src/local.rs` L24; `HALF_WORLD_SIZE` = 128, private, L21). `sim` parses to (-128, -128), packed 0, only under the crate's `sim` feature (L357-359), which no build here enables (`cargo tree -e features`, review of 2026-11-14). For `i32::MIN` under `W` or `N` the -n-1 overflows: a panic with overflow checks on (the test profile), an error in release (the root profile sets no overflow-checks). The two `s[..]` slices link `core::str::slice_error_fail`, and with it `<char as Debug>::fmt` and the `grapheme_extend`, `cn_planes_0_3` and `white_space` lookup tables, into the release wasm. A wasm cannot travel through the link as a file: the bytes arrive as text (UTF-8 mangles bytes >= 0x80, as V74 shows for the client's mirror), under a name the loader does not ask for. These are the loader's own `getUsed` deltas: the main.js evaluation and the Memory read before its first reading, and the end-of-tick save, are outside them (unmeasured); the boot line's log (with the globals report) is inside `compile`, and the sentinel `require` inside `load+crc`. Rules: the free-tier limit is 20 (V35); tickLimit = min(500, bucket + limit) and the refill is bucket + limit − ceil(used) (V37); the kill is at tickLimit + 5 ms of wall time (V105). So both boot ticks cost less than the limit, and a boot never lowers the bucket at the free tier (from the numbers). From bucket 480 every boot tick gets tickLimit 500, so a gate above 480 adds no protection and only defers the boot (from the rule); no code runs while a boot is deferred, and a deferred tick is billed about 1 CPU, so from an empty bucket the wait is about 26 ticks at 480 and 79 at 1500 (inferred). The first boot tick runs outside the loader's `running` flag, so a kill there is no incident and is retried whenever the bucket reaches the gate (js/src/loader.ts `boot`); the second (instantiate and the first loop) runs under it, so a kill there is an aborted incident with halt and backoff. Gate set to 480 = `CPU_TICK_LIMIT_MAX` − 20, the user's choice of three options (480, 200, keep 1500) on 2026-11-14. At the V76 upload budget (a wasm of about 1.5 MB) the first boot tick projects to about 70 CPU, far below 500 (inferred, linear in size). With CPU Unlock (a limit above 20) the full tickLimit starts at a lower bucket, so 480 still gives it. | official MMO shard3, free tier; plain build at ADR-015 (wasm 230513 bytes) | V149, V35, V37, V105; js/src/loader.ts | 2026-11-14 | verified (the costs and the rules); inferred (the deferral ticks, the growth projection) | `LOADER_MMO.boot_bucket_min` (config.rs, TUNING); test `boot_gate_is_the_lowest_bucket_with_the_full_tick_limit` |

## H. P2 verification (2026-11-14 onward)

Method: as section G. Movement (P2.1): two screeps-verifier researchers read the pinned engine (a90d26f) raw and the local screeps-game-api 0.23.5 and js-sys 0.3.107 sources; line numbers from fetched engine files are hand counts and may be off by a few. RCL1–3 opening (P2.4, V182–V202): four researcher and skeptic pairs (work actions; leftovers; sources and bodies; find and sites) read the pinned engine through a summarizing fetch, so no engine file was read byte-exact in that pass and line numbers marked "about" are approximate; the crate rows come from the local 0.23.5 registry copy. A row there is `verified` only where both readers read the point and agree, no row is raised above the researcher's own label, the skeptic's reading wins where the two differ, and each row names the points one reader alone read.

The only same-tick counter, `createdConstructionSites` (reset in `exports.make`, raised after an OK call), feeds only the `MAX_CONSTRUCTION_SITES` check (ERR_FULL counts the user's sites in all rooms). ## I. P3 verification (2026-11-16 onward)

Method: as section H. Static miners (P3.1): the values come from the pinned screeps-game-api 0.23.5 source in the local cargo registry, read directly; V131's programmatic comparison against engine common 2.16.0 covers them. The mechanics are derived from rows already verified in section H; nothing new was fetched from the engine for V211 and V212. V213 and the live part of V214 are the user's console reads on shard3 (STATUS log); V214's code and V215 were read byte-exact from the engine at a90d26f with curl (sha256 prefixes in their rows). The link network (P3.2): three screeps-verifier researchers, each with a skeptic who re-read the load-bearing lines with curl at a90d26f (V216, V218 to V220); the processor's link files and processor.js were read byte-exact in the main session (V217).

## J. P4 verification (2026-11-17 onward)

Method: the terrain corpus (P4.1). V224 to V231 were read by the screeps-verifier subagent through WebFetch, which summarizes, so the quotes are paraphrases, at the branch tips screeps/common 2fb779b26eef9b4b0f412584f6bd47c897949766 (package 2.16.1), screeps/backend-local 99dde063821f7549e713dde022ab8579ab2b1d5f, screeps/driver cf63d8adf902663e2ebddd7f8c5b7baa425dc928 and screeps/engine a90d26fc9eb943147799e65d0bf9d4d0d79a2722. The official server's map and storage code is closed, so every generator and backend row is inferred for the MMO; V233 records what the MMO data itself showed. V232 and V233 are this session's own observations.

Common's `encodeTerrain` loops y outer and x inner; `decodeTerrain` and `checkTerrain` read `charAt(y * 50 + x)`. P4.2 method: the crate rows come from the pinned screeps-game-api 0.23.5 source in the local cargo registry, read directly. For the engine rows (V237 to V243), the files were fetched from GitHub at a90d26f and hashed against the local Steam client's @screeps/engine 4.3.0. All ten files are byte-identical: utils.js 8711758a18de, towers/attack.js 607ac8a3047e, heal.js 991e8f9a4043, repair.js 5c7158d2fdcb, labs/run-reaction.js e0f2b802ec87, boost-creep.js 104929c53d90, ramparts/tick.js 6ac3ac4a46af, roads/tick.js 775a3157b353, creeps/rangedAttack.js ae39394f026e, rangedMassAttack.js eab478d13ff6 (sha256 prefixes). The quoted lines were then read verbatim. V244 and V245 rest on summarizing reads by the screeps-verifier subagent and on community sources.

(1) For every type but road and container at x or y of 1 or 48, the three tiles beside it on the border must all be terrain walls. The ifs run in the order x 1, x 48, y 1, y 48, so at a corner the y rule replaces the x rule: (1, 1) checks (0, 0), (1, 0) and (2, 0). This covers ramparts and walls. (2) On terrain, only a road may stand on a wall tile, and an extractor skips the terrain check. (3) On objects, a structure of the same type on the tile refuses, as does any construction site. An extractor needs a mineral there and no extractor. Every type but rampart and road is refused by any object on the tile that is not a rampart or road and has a CONSTRUCTION_COST, so a container blocks an extension, while a road or rampart may go over any structure. (4) x or y of 0 or 49 refuses. Not known: whether `rustedscreep_wasm_1` syncs and `require`s on the MMO (V258 suggests it, for the API path), whether the files of one push always arrive together, and whether the console can call `require`. Safe default until checked: no module file above 905709 bytes (tighter than V257's 1,000,000, which rests on the inferred cause); the optional probe of ADR-028 (the live build plus a dummy `rustedscreep_wasm_1.js` of about 650 KB) or the first split push settles the first two. | official MMO, shard3, GitHub link | user console 2026-11-18; V145, V257, V258 | 2026-11-18 | unverified; the per-push limit and the digit-suffixed name superseded 2026-11-18 → V260 | `MODULE_MAX_BYTES` in js/tools/lib/wasm-text.mjs (ADR-028) |

So the sync has no 1 MB cap on the whole push (at least up to 1399292 bytes), a module named after `rustedscreep_wasm_1.js` syncs as `rustedscreep_wasm_1` and `require` returns its export string, and both parts of this push arrived in the same sync (one observation). ## K. P5 verification (2026-11-20 onward)

| # | Group | Parameter | Finding | Applies to | Source | Date | Confidence | Constant / code location |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V261 | tooling | `Creep.ticksToLive` through screeps-game-api 0.23.5 and wasm-bindgen 0.2.130 (P5.2) | The crate declares `#[wasm_bindgen(structural, method, getter = ticksToLive)] fn ticks_to_live_internal(this: &Creep) -> Option<u32>` (src/objects/impls/creep.rs L84-85), wrapped by `pub fn ticks_to_live(&self) -> Option<u32>` (L271-276). The glue wasm-bindgen generated for this build (dist/main.js, the import `__wbg_ticks_to_live_internal_819d2d1b62b60d7d`) reads `const ret = arg0.ticksToLive; return isLikeNone(ret) ? Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER : (ret) >>> 0;`, with `isLikeNone(x)` being `x === undefined \|\| x === null`. So an undefined getter (a spawning creep, V198) arrives as `None` and never traps, a number arrives as its uint32 (the engine's value is a positive integer, V198), and each read is one crossing. The snapshot keeps `Game.time` plus the first number as the creep's death tick and reads no more. That the tick stays fixed rests on V170 (creeps/tick.js sets `ageTime` only while it is unset) and on the bot never calling `renewCreep`, which raises it (renew-creep.js not read). | crate 0.23.5; wasm-bindgen 0.2.130 | the pinned crate's source in the local cargo registry (screeps-game-api-0.23.5/src/objects/impls/creep.rs); the generated glue in dist/main.js (`npm run build`, 2026-11-20) | 2026-11-20 | verified (the binding and the glue, both read here); inferred (that nothing but `renewCreep` moves `ageTime`) | rustedscreep-wasm snapshot.rs `push_creep`; rustedscreep-core `snapshot::Creeps::set_death`, `spawning::replace` |
| V262 | creep | `Creep.ticksToLive` falls by one a tick on the MMO (P5.2; supports V198's inference and V261's fixed death tick) | Two console reads of every own creep's `ticksToLive` (`Object.values(Game.creeps).map(...)`), 64 ticks apart on shard3, showed all 7 creeps exactly 64 lower: 124 → 60, 396 → 332, 455 → 391, 568 → 504, 790 → 726, 1024 → 960, 1059 → 995. So `ageTime` stayed fixed for each, and `Game.time` plus `ticksToLive` is the same tick at both reads, as the snapshot's kept death tick assumes. The first and last visible values (V198) are not settled by it. | MMO shard3, build fed995490f52-dirty-53658e17 | user console, Game.time about 84391650 to 84391712 | 2026-11-20 | verified (live, one pair of reads) | rustedscreep-core `snapshot::Creeps::set_death`; rustedscreep-wasm snapshot.rs `push_creep` |
